            <rect x="280" y="276" width="224" height="7" rx="3.5" fill="var(--line)"/><rect x="280" y="276" width="130" height="7" rx="3.5" fill="var(--amber)"/>
            <text x="508" y="270" text-anchor="end" class="svmute">58</text>
          </g>
          <text x="266" y="312" class="svmute" font-size="10">Each answer adds weight × raw score to one or more axes. Skipped branches add nothing.</text>
          <path d="M522 182 C544 182 542 176 556 176" fill="none" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-width="1.8" marker-end="url(#dp2)" class="flowline"/>
          <path d="M638 190 A 64 64 0 0 1 766 190" fill="none" stroke="var(--line)" stroke-width="14" stroke-linecap="round"/>
          <path d="M638 190 A 64 64 0 0 1 736 136" fill="none" stroke="var(--accent)" stroke-width="14" stroke-linecap="round"/>
          <text x="702" y="168" text-anchor="middle" class="svlabel" font-size="30">68</text>
          <text x="702" y="188" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">out of 100</text>
          <text x="632" y="208" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">0</text>
          <text x="772" y="208" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">100</text>
          <rect x="562" y="226" width="282" height="38" rx="9" fill="var(--amber-bg)"/>
          <text x="703" y="240" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--amber)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">BAND · conversion-limited</text>
          <text x="703" y="256" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">your weakest axis names the band</text>
          <rect x="562" y="276" width="282" height="150" rx="12" fill="var(--accent)"/>
          <text x="582" y="300" class="svwhite" font-size="12">RECOMMENDED FOR YOU</text>
          <text x="582" y="326" class="svwhite" font-size="14">The 90-day conversion sprint</text>
          <text x="582" y="348" class="svwhite2" font-size="11">Because your retention is already strong,</text>
          <text x="582" y="366" class="svwhite2" font-size="11">the fastest money is in the step before it.</text>
          <line x1="582" y1="384" x2="824" y2="384" stroke="rgba(255,255,255,.28)"/>
          <text x="582" y="406" class="svwhite2" font-size="11">Their own words, echoed back →  one CTA</text>
          <rect x="16" y="450" width="828" height="86" rx="12" fill="var(--panel-2)" stroke="var(--line-2)"/>
          <text x="36" y="476" class="svlabel" font-size="11.5">THE RULE IS STORED, NOT DECIDED</text>
          <text x="36" y="496" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">score 0–40 → entry rung · 41–75 → core rung · 76–100 → flagship rung. Written down once, applied identically to everyone.</text>
          <text x="36" y="514" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">That is what makes it a machine: same inputs, same prescription, every time — and a rule you can A/B test instead of a hunch you can't.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>The band is the story, the score is the hook.</b> A number gets attention. The <em>band</em> — "you're conversion-limited" — is what makes the recommendation feel inevitable rather than sold.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box eg"><div class="h">◆ Example · the same engine in four wrappers</div>
        <p><strong>In a live session:</strong> three polls, and the poll answers segment your follow-up. <strong>On a registration page:</strong> two qualification fields that route registrants into different reminder sequences. <strong>On a reverse squeeze bridge:</strong> one question between Part 1 and Part 2 that decides which Part 2 they get. <strong>As a standalone assessment:</strong> the full scored quiz with a gauge and a paywall. Same ask → score → band → prescribe. The only thing that changes is how many questions you can get away with.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 2.2</span><b>Design Your Diagnostic</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Name 3–5 score axes.</strong> These are the dimensions your offer actually improves. If your offer fixes conversion, one axis is conversion. Don't invent axes you can't help with.</li>
            <li><strong>Write 6–12 agreement statements</strong> and tag each to an axis. Statements, not questions: <em>"I know which step of my funnel leaks the most"</em> — agree/disagree.</li>
            <li><strong>Write the three band labels</strong> — one per likely weak axis. These become headlines later.</li>
            <li><strong>Write the band → rung rule</strong> and commit to it in writing.</li>
          </ol>
          <p>Keep it short. Every extra question costs you completions, and a 12-statement diagnostic that people finish beats a 30-statement one they abandon.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You</span><span><i>Tool</i>Doc, then the spec tomorrow</span><span><i>Time</i>45 min</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Axes · statements · bands · the mapping rule</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Every statement maps to exactly one axis you can improve</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · the diagnostic</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>It becomes <em>polls</em> — three of them, run live. Poll answers are the highest-intent segmentation signal you'll get all week, so tag them into the CRM the moment they're cast.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>It becomes the <em>daily survey</em>. You get five chances to ask instead of one — so spread it, and let each day's answers personalise the next day's output.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>It becomes the <em>opt-in bridge</em>. One or two questions maximum, placed between Part 1 and Part 2, where the value has already been delivered and they owe you an answer.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> a book-a-call funnel puts the diagnostic on the <b>booking form</b>, where it doubles as call prep · tripwire and free-plus-shipping funnels diagnose <b>after</b> purchase, to pick which upsell to show · a community funnel diagnoses on join, to route people to the right channel.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d1-step-2-build-the-diagnose-then-prescribe-engine"></div>
<h3 id="d1-step-3-put-it-all-in-one-file">Step 3 — Put It All in One File</h3>
      <p>Now the part that turns a document into an engine. Everything you just wrote — brand, ICP, offer, pricing, the statements, the bands, the rule — goes into <strong>one structured file.</strong> Not five documents. One.</p>
      <p>Why it matters: a page built from a file can be changed by editing the file. A page built by hand can only be changed by whoever built it, in the place they built it, if they remember where. <strong>One file is the difference between a funnel you can tune and a funnel you have to rebuild.</strong></p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">One spec file → one renderer → every asset, and every experiment</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 512" role="img" aria-label="Spec-driven architecture. Five context blocks feed a single funnel spec file listing its keys. Below, an experiments lane shows variant A and variant B stored in the same file, split fifty-fifty across sessions, with the winner written back into the file.">

          </g>
          <path d="M735 370 C735 344 640 336 500 336 L360 336" fill="none" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-width="1.6" stroke-dasharray="5 6" marker-end="url(#sp2)" opacity=".85"/>
          <text x="548" y="352" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">back into the file — so the funnel improves itself</text>
          <text x="36" y="444" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">One change per leak. Never two at once, or you can't attribute the result. Let each arm reach a meaningful sample before you decide —</text>
          <text x="36" y="462" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">a one-day blip is noise. And log every result, including the losers, so you never re-run an experiment you've already lost.</text>
          <text x="36" y="480" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">This lane is why Day 3 exists. Build it now and the optimisation loop has somewhere to write.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>You don't have to write the file by hand.</b> Tomorrow you describe it and your AI writes it. But you have to know <em>what belongs in it</em> — which is exactly what today produced.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box deep"><div class="h">◆ The rule that makes this work</div>
        <p><strong>Nothing per-brand gets hardcoded.</strong> If a headline, a price, a question or an event name lives inside the page code rather than the spec, you've just created work for your future self — and guaranteed that changing it needs a developer. Copy, prices, questions, score rules, event names and experiment variants all live in the file. The code only knows how to <em>render</em>.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 2.3</span><b>Write the Spec Outline — Keys Only</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>You're not writing JSON today. You're writing the <strong>outline</strong>: the list of keys and what each one holds. Nine top-level sections is plenty:</p>
          <ul>
            <li><code>brand</code> · <code>icp</code> · <code>offer</code> · <code>pricing</code> — straight from your five blocks</li>
            <li><code>stages</code> — the six from the master map, each with its screens</li>
            <li><code>scoreModel</code> — the axes, statements and the band rule from Task 2.2</li>
            <li><code>copy</code> — headlines and CTAs, one per screen</li>
            <li><code>analytics</code> — the event names from your Day-1 arithmetic</li>
            <li><code>experiments</code> — empty today, filled from Day 3 onward</li>
          </ul>
          <p>Tomorrow's build reads this outline. An hour here saves a day there.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · your AI drafts it</span><span><i>Tool</i>Claude Code, or any AI</span><span><i>Time</i>30 min</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Spec outline, nine sections</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Nothing in it is a design decision — it's all content and rules</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · the spec</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>Add an <code>event</code> section: date, time, duration, timezone handling, and the replay window. Everything in S3 reads its timings from that one place — change the date once, every reminder moves.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Add a <code>days[]</code> array — five entries, each with its session, its survey questions, and the output it generates. The daily loop is data, not five hand-built pages.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Add <code>contentParts[]</code> with watch-depth thresholds. Your whole branching logic keys off "how far did they get", so those thresholds belong in the spec where you can tune them.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> product-launch funnels add a dated <b>open/close cart window</b> · free-plus-shipping adds <b>shipping zones and COGS</b> so margin is computable · JV funnels add a <b>partners[]</b> block with revenue-share percentages, because attribution has to be unambiguous before money moves.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Your Day 1 output — half two</div>
        <p>A <strong>conversion engine</strong>: five context blocks filled, a diagnostic designed (axes, statements, bands, mapping rule), and a spec outline that tomorrow's build reads directly. This is the single source of truth every asset on Day 2 gets generated from.</p></div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Tonight's homework · 30 minutes</div>
        <p>Gather your proof assets into one folder — screenshots, numbers, testimonials, logos, links. Tomorrow you'll be generating pages that need them, and hunting for a testimonial mid-build is how people lose an hour. Name each file after the objection it answers.</p></div>
    <div class="vipslot" data-step="d1-step-3-put-it-all-in-one-file"></div>

26:T1a06,<section style="--hue:#EC4899;--hue-ink:color-mix(in srgb,#EC4899 70%,var(--ink))" id="d2">
<div class="chap"><span class="no">Day 2</span><span class="tag">Funnel Elements · Every Page, Template and Stage</span></div>
      <h2>Build Every Element — The Whole Funnel, Assembled</h2>
      <p class="lead">Today the engine becomes assets. You work stage by stage, S1 to S6, and at the end of it every page, every message and every automation trigger exists as something you could hand to someone else.</p>
      <div class="box why"><div class="h">◆ Build in this order, and only this order</div>
        <p><strong>The funnel core before the traffic.</strong> Build S2 through S5 first — the pages, the sequences, the close — then attach S1 on top. People do it backwards, switch on traffic into a half-built funnel, and burn a list they can never un-burn. Traffic is the cheapest thing to add and the most expensive thing to waste.</p></div>
      <h3 id="d2-the-path-a-visitor-actually-moves-through">The Path a Visitor Actually Moves Through</h3>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">The path a visitor actually moves through — with the branch that matters</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 106 860 247" role="img" aria-label="Journey flowchart: organic and paid lanes converge into landing, thank-you and content pages, then a ready-or-not decision branches to a sales page and checkout, or into a nurture loop that feeds back.">
          <defs>

<h3 id="d2-s1-the-traffic-engine">S1 — The Traffic Engine</h3>
      <p>Five channels. Each one has hard operating limits, and each limit exists because someone learned it the expensive way — a burned domain, a restricted account, a list that can never be emailed again.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Daily operating limits, and the weekly scale-or-fix gate</div>

          <text x="430" y="530" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">Re-measure before touching another.</text>
          <rect x="576" y="416" width="268" height="128" rx="12" fill="var(--pink-bg)"/>

          <text x="710" y="462" text-anchor="middle" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">open &lt; 15%  OR  CTR &lt; 1%</text>
          <text x="710" y="479" text-anchor="middle" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">OR  bounce &gt; 5%</text>
          <line x1="604" y1="494" x2="816" y2="494" stroke="var(--pink)" opacity=".3"/>
          <text x="710" y="514" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">Stop sending on that inbox now.</text>
          <text x="710" y="530" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">Low open = deliverability. Low CTR = offer.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>The diagnosis is in which number is low.</b> A weak open rate is a deliverability problem — a domain and warm-up issue. A weak click-through is an <em>offer</em> problem, and no amount of inbox tuning will fix it.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.1</span><b>Stand Up the Sending Infrastructure Before You Write a Word</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li>Buy <strong>separate domains</strong> for outreach. Never your main brand domain — if it gets flagged, your invoices stop arriving too.</li>
            <li>Point DNS at your provider and set <strong>SPF, DKIM and DMARC</strong>. All three. Missing any one of them halves your inbox placement.</li>
            <li>Create your sending accounts and put them straight into <strong>warm-up for 2–3 weeks.</strong> Yes, that's before the workshop ends. Start today and they're ready when your funnel is.</li>
            <li>Validate your list. Target under 1% bounce, and reject any purchased file with more than 1–2% duplicates.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You</span><span><i>Tool</i>Domain registrar · DNS · sending platform</span><span><i>Time</i>90 min setup, then 2–3 weeks of waiting</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Authenticated domains in warm-up</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>A test send lands in the primary inbox, not promotions</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.2</span><b>Write Two Variants per Channel, Not One</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>One variant gives you a result. Two give you a <em>comparison</em> — and a comparison is the only thing that teaches you anything. Generate both from your conversion engine so they share the offer and differ only in angle:</p>
          <ul>
            <li><strong>Variant A · benefit-led</strong> — lead with the dream outcome from your value equation.</li>
            <li><strong>Variant B · problem-led</strong> — lead with the verbatim objection from your ICP card.</li>
          </ul>
          <p>Same for ads, same for LinkedIn messages, same for content hooks. And run every link through your UTM scheme — a channel you can't attribute is a channel you can't defend at budget time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · AI drafts from the engine</span><span><i>Tool</i>Claude Code, or any AI</span><span><i>Time</i>60 min</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>2 variants × every live channel, UTM-tagged</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Both variants trace back to a block in your engine</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · traffic</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>Traffic is <em>dated</em> — every asset carries a date and expires. Run the invite push in a concentrated window, and change the event date a few minutes before you go live so the listing re-ranks for the next cycle.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Traffic is <em>cohort-shaped</em>, and peer referral is a real channel: participants recruit participants. Give them a shareable link with their own tag on it from day one.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Traffic points at <em>content</em>, not a form — so it never expires and it accumulates. The same link works for the next twelve months, which changes what a click is worth.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> product launches concentrate everything into a <b>dated open-cart window</b> · hub-and-spoke and community funnels run permanently-on evergreen traffic · JV funnels replace your traffic entirely with a <b>partner's list</b>, which means your real S1 work is partner recruitment, not ad creative.</div>
      </div>

<h3 id="d2-s2-the-landing-and-registration-page">S2 — The Landing and Registration Page</h3>
      <p>One page: the <strong>Tantra Pro Funnel</strong> page — branded, proof-led, form above the fold. What you A/B test is the headline (benefit-led against problem-led), never a second, thinner page.</p>

          <text x="556" y="440" class="svsub" font-size="10">Every block answers one objection</text>
          <text x="556" y="454" class="svsub" font-size="10">and drives one action.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>One page, built from one spec.</b> Every block is switched on because it answers an objection or earns the action. If a block does neither, it comes out.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Pro tip · the form abandon number is the one nobody watches</div>
        <p>People obsess over the conversion rate and ignore <strong>form abandonment</strong> — the visitors who started typing and stopped. That number tells you something conversion rate can't: they <em>wanted</em> to, and something in the form stopped them. Usually it's a field you didn't need. Every field you remove is a measurable lift.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.3</span><b>Build the Tantra Pro Funnel Page and the Thank-You Page</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Two pages, generated from the spec:</p>
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Tantra Pro Funnel page</strong> — nine blocks, form above the fold at 1366×768.</li>
            <li><strong>Thank-you</strong> — and this one earns its keep. It confirms, it adds to calendar, it asks them to join your community group, and it sets the expectation of what arrives next. A thank-you page that only says "thanks" is a wasted asset.</li>
          </ol>
          <p>Then check every page at <strong>375px, 768px and 1280px</strong> before you call it done. Most funnel traffic is mobile, and most funnels are built on a laptop.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · AI builds from the spec</span><span><i>Tool</i>Claude Code + your host</span><span><i>Time</i>3–5 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>2 live pages under one URL scheme</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>A real registration lands in your CRM, from a phone</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · the capture page</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>The date block is load-bearing — timezone handling, an add-to-calendar button, and a countdown. Get the timezone wrong and your show-up rate collapses for reasons you'll never diagnose.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>The page is a <em>checkout</em>, not a form. Wallets first, card below, order summary on top — and the three-day commitment stated plainly, because setting the expectation is what protects your completion rate.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>There is no form on the entry page at all. The page <em>is</em> Part 1, and the opt-in bridge only appears once they've consumed enough of it to owe you an answer.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> book-a-call funnels replace the form with a <b>calendar embed plus qualification fields</b> · free-plus-shipping needs a full address form, so every removable field matters twice as much · a final-funnel has no page at all — the "capture" is a reply in an inbox.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d2-s2-the-landing-and-registration-page"></div>

<h3 id="d2-s3-the-nurture-engine-where-show-up-is-won">S3 — The Nurture Engine (Where Show-Up Is Won)</h3>
      <p>Here's the number nobody wants to hear: <strong>roughly half the people who register never turn up.</strong> Not because your content is bad — they haven't seen it yet. Because life happened and nothing reminded them hard enough.</p>
      <p>Nurture is the cheapest lift in the entire funnel. You already paid to acquire these people. Every reminder that converts a registration into an attendee costs you almost nothing and is worth exactly as much as a new lead.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">The Tantra Pro Flow — six touches before, two branches after</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 540" role="img" aria-label="Nurture flowchart. A pre-event column of six timed touches from registration confirmation through T minus seven days, T minus twenty-four hours, T minus one hour, T minus fifteen minutes and going live, each tagged with its channels. These lead into the live session and an attended-or-not decision. The attended branch has four follow-ups from the offer through a last call; the no-show branch has four, from an expiring replay link through a final notice and then a thirty-day nurture. Both branches merge into the fifty-two week cadence.">

        <div class="cap"><b>The no-show branch is worth more than it looks.</b> These people registered — they raised their hand and then life got in the way. They are not cold, and treating them like a lost cause is the most common unforced error in the whole funnel.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Do this · send the T−24h reminder at 8am in their timezone</div>
        <p>Not 8am in yours. A reminder that lands at 3am is a reminder that gets buried under everything that arrived after it. If your tool can't send by recipient timezone, that's a reason to change tools — this single setting moves show-up more than most copy changes will.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.4</span><b>Write the Tantra Pro Flow and Set the Send Windows</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Generate all of it from the engine, then set the mechanics by hand:</p>
          <ul>
            <li><strong>Word counts matter.</strong> The T−24h email can be 180–220 words. The T−1h is 80–100. The "we're live" is 50–70 and contains one link. People skim in inverse proportion to urgency.</li>
            <li><strong>Every message has exactly one link.</strong> Two links is a decision, and a decision at T−15 minutes is a lost attendee.</li>
            <li><strong>Run every draft through a spam checker</strong> before it goes near a real list.</li>
            <li><strong>Set the branch tags</strong> — registered, attended, no-show, replay-watched. Everything downstream keys off these, so name them once and never rename them.</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · AI drafts, you set timings</span><span><i>Tool</i>Email + messaging platform</span><span><i>Time</i>3 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>14-node Tantra Pro Flow, live</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>You register as a test contact and receive every touch, on time</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · nurture</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>Purely time-triggered off one date. Build it once and every future run reuses it — change the date in the spec and all six touches move together.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Five nested copies of the same shape, plus the daily delivery loop: recap → the artifact they generated → what tomorrow needs. Non-completion on day two is your early-warning signal for churn.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Not time-triggered at all — <em>depth-triggered</em>. Under 25% of Part 1 gets a re-engage; 25–75% gets the missing-piece nudge; over 75% goes straight to the Part 2 bridge.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> book-a-call funnels replace show-up nurture with <b>no-show rebooking</b>, which recovers a meaningful slice of bookings · product launches run a dated open-cart countdown · free-plus-shipping switches nurture to <b>post-purchase</b> entirely, because the sale already happened.</div>
      </div>

<h3 id="d2-s4-the-live-delivery">S4 — The Live Delivery</h3>
      <p>Everything so far was preparation for sixty minutes. Run those sixty minutes off a script and a clock, not off vibes — because the moments that produce bookings happen at specific times, and if you're improvising you'll miss them.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Run of show — sixty minutes, four lanes, nothing improvised</div>

Direct message lane: three timed one-to-one messages at three, fifteen and forty minutes. Tech lane: recording on at the start, attendee counts logged every five minutes, replay exported at the end.             <rect x="180" y="350" width="152" height="108" rx="10" class="node"/><text x="194" y="372" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Content moderator</text><text x="194" y="392" class="svmute" font-size="9">Feeds the host questions,</text><text x="194" y="406" class="svmute" font-size="9">signals pace and breaks,</text><text x="194" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="9">and runs the polls on</text><text x="194" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9">the clock.</text>
            <rect x="344" y="350" width="152" height="108" rx="10" class="node"/><text x="358" y="372" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Tech moderator</text><text x="358" y="392" class="svmute" font-size="9">Owns the platform.</text><text x="358" y="406" class="svmute" font-size="9">Promotes panelists,</text><text x="358" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="9">answers "I can't hear you"</text><text x="358" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9">in private chat.</text>
            <rect x="508" y="350" width="152" height="108" rx="10" class="node"/><text x="522" y="372" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Chat &amp; Q&amp;A</text><text x="522" y="392" class="svmute" font-size="9">Posts the CTA link on</text><text x="522" y="406" class="svmute" font-size="9">cadence, removes spam,</text><text x="522" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="9">curates questions so the</text><text x="522" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9">best ones surface.</text>
            <rect x="672" y="350" width="152" height="108" rx="10" class="node"/><text x="686" y="372" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">DM operator</text><text x="686" y="392" class="svmute" font-size="9">Sends the three timed</text><text x="686" y="406" class="svmute" font-size="9">messages and captures</text><text x="686" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="9">replies straight into</text><text x="686" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9">the CRM.</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>Do a full dry run.</b> Same platform, same slides, same polls, a test attendee on a second device. Every failure you find in rehearsal costs you nothing; the same failure live costs you the room.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.5</span><b>Write the Run of Show, the Script and the Three Polls</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Three artifacts, all generated from the engine and then hand-tuned:</p>
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Run of show</strong> — the timeline above, with your actual slide numbers written against each marker.</li>
            <li><strong>The script</strong> — not word-for-word, but every transition written out. The three places people fumble are the open, the move from teaching into the offer, and the close of Q&amp;A. Write those three verbatim.</li>
            <li><strong>Three polls</strong> — these are your diagnostic from Day 1, in live form. Poll answers are the strongest intent signal of the whole session, so make sure they land in the CRM tagged, not in a screenshot.</li>
          </ol>
          <p>Then assign the five roles to actual names. A role with nobody's name on it is a role nobody does.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You + your team</span><span><i>Tool</i>Slides · your session platform</span><span><i>Time</i>6–8 h including the deck</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Run of show · script · 3 polls · named roles</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>A full dry run completes with no unknowns left</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · delivery</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>One shot. The offer lands once, near the end, and has to survive being heard without scroll-back — so repeat the link in chat on a cadence and put it on the slide too.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Five shots, and the offer is only on day five. Days one to four have to <em>earn</em> day five by shipping something real each time — the artifact is the proof that the method works.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Nothing is live at all. Part 2 is recorded and delivered instantly, which means the "run of show" is an edit decision list — where the proof lands, where the offer lands, and where you cut.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> product launches run this four times across a video sequence · a sideways sales letter spreads the same beats over multiple pages instead of minutes · book-a-call funnels have no delivery stage at all — the call <b>is</b> the delivery, which is why the qualification form matters so much more there.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d2-s4-the-live-delivery"></div>

2b:T2d62,<section style="--hue:#EC4899;--hue-ink:color-mix(in srgb,#EC4899 70%,var(--ink))">
<h3 id="d2-s5-the-close-offer-booking-payment">S5 — The Close: Offer, Booking, Payment</h3>
      <p>A booked call is not revenue. A proposal is not revenue. Money in the account is revenue, and between the offer and the money there are five places people quietly fall out. Build all five now, not after you lose someone at each.</p>
      <p>Stage 5 is where attention becomes revenue, and every mechanism feeds the same eleven-stage pipeline: Form Filled → Eval Call Scheduled → Qualified or Unqualified → Sales Call Held → Follow-Up → Proposal Sent → Proposal Accepted → Payment Completed → Onboarding → Delivery, plus a No Show exit off the booked call. Unqualified and No Show recover people instead of losing them. Each stage is entered by an event, not by someone remembering to drag a card, and each one has an owner, a clock and a follow-up that fires on its own. Build it once in GoHighLevel or in the starter CRM; only the stages in front of Form Filled change with your mechanism.</p>

        <div class="cap"><b>Assets this stage needs before launch:</b> discovery script · demo script · close script · objection battlecards · proposal template · pricing calculator · no-show recovery sequence · welcome and onboarding sequence.</div>

      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.6</span><b>Build the Close Assets and Wire the Booking Flow</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Generated from the engine, in this order — each one blocks the next:</p>
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Booking page</strong> with 3–6 qualification fields. Those fields are your Day-2 diagnostic, and they double as call prep.</li>
            <li><strong>Confirmation + two reminders</strong>, T−24h and T−1h. A booked call with no reminders is a coin flip.</li>
            <li><strong>Three scripts</strong> — discovery, demo, close. Plus battlecards for the two objections you hear most.</li>
            <li><strong>Proposal template</strong> and a pricing calculator, so a proposal takes ten minutes, not an afternoon.</li>
            <li><strong>Checkout</strong> — wallets first, card below, order summary on top, and a server-side webhook confirming the charge. Never trust the browser to tell you a payment succeeded.</li>
            <li><strong>Welcome and onboarding sequence</strong>, triggered by that webhook. The first 48 hours after payment decide your churn.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · AI drafts scripts and pages</span><span><i>Tool</i>Booking tool · payments · CRM</span><span><i>Time</i>5–7 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Booking → reminders → scripts → proposal → checkout → onboarding</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>A real test purchase completes and the onboarding email arrives</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · the close</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>The close is a <em>booked call</em> for high ticket, or direct checkout below roughly a thousand. Above that, a page rarely carries the objection alone.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Two closes. The entry checkout on day zero — which is really a qualifier — and the ascension offer on day five, sold to people who have already paid you once and seen the method work.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>The close is a page, routed by engagement depth. Deep consumers see the premium branch; shallow ones see the low-ticket branch. Same page template, two prices, decided by data.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> tripwire and unlock-all funnels close <b>inside the checkout</b> with an order bump — no second page at all · downsell funnels close on the <b>decline</b>, which means the second offer must exist before launch · JV funnels need the revenue split resolved at checkout, automatically, or you'll be reconciling spreadsheets for months.</div>
      </div>

2c:T586,<section style="--hue:#EC4899;--hue-ink:color-mix(in srgb,#EC4899 70%,var(--ink))">
<h3 id="d2-s6-what-happens-to-everyone-who-didn-t-buy">S6 — What Happens to Everyone Who Didn't Buy</h3>
      <p>Most of your list, on any given cycle, will not buy. That is normal and it is not failure — it's timing. The mistake is letting them go quiet, because a warm list that goes silent for six months is a cold list again.</p>
      <p>Most of your list, on any cycle, won't buy yet. Stage 6 keeps them warm for a year: one weekly email that teaches your method a step at a time and proves it with stories, a short WhatsApp message beside every email, and a rare SMS when something is time-critical. The newsletter is drafted by an AI every week from your own context and that week's news; you edit, approve and schedule it. Everyone enters through one tag, and anyone who replies re-enters the pipeline.</p>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Do this · define the four tags now and never rename them</div>
        <p><strong>registered · attended · no-show · purchased.</strong> Every automation, every report and every audience you build for the rest of the year keys off these four. Name them today, write them into the spec, and treat renaming one as a breaking change — because it is.</p></div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d2-s6-what-happens-to-everyone-who-didn-t-buy"></div>

2d:T38ab,<section style="--hue:#EC4899;--hue-ink:color-mix(in srgb,#EC4899 70%,var(--ink))">
<h3 id="d2-the-reverse-squeeze-funnel-end-to-end">The Reverse Squeeze Funnel, End to End</h3>
      <p>Everything above assumed a dated event. A reverse squeeze has no date, no registration and no live moment — and it still runs all six stages. Here's the whole thing, at the same depth, because "it's just a landing page with a video" is how people build one that doesn't work.</p>
      <p>The core idea: <strong>you give the valuable thing away before you ask for anything.</strong> Not a teaser. Not a preview. The actual thing. The opt-in comes afterwards, and it converts far better because by then you've earned it.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Reverse squeeze — all six stages, no date, no form up front</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 758" role="img" aria-label="Reverse squeeze funnel across six stages. Stage one: the same five traffic channels, but every link points at content rather than a form. Stage two: Part One delivered openly with watch-depth tracking and an opt-in bridge that appears at a threshold. Stage three: depth-triggered nurture with three branches by consumption level. Stage four: Part Two gated and delivered instantly. Stage five: a backend offer routed by engagement score into a low-ticket or premium branch. Stage six: a dual-path growth engine. A loop returns re-engaged contacts to the top.">

            <rect x="576" y="192" width="266" height="52" rx="9" fill="var(--teal-bg)"/><text x="590" y="212" fill="var(--teal)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">The opt-in bridge</text><text x="590" y="228" class="svmute" font-size="9">appears only past the threshold.</text><text x="590" y="240" class="svmute" font-size="9">"Part 2 goes deeper — where do I send it?"</text>

          </g>
          <rect x="44" y="518" width="800" height="114" rx="14" fill="var(--panel-2)" stroke="var(--line-2)"/>

          </g>
          <rect x="44" y="652" width="800" height="90" rx="14" fill="var(--panel-2)" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-opacity=".5"/>
          <rect x="60" y="664" width="32" height="21" rx="6" fill="var(--teal)"/><text x="76" y="675" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite" font-size="11.5">S6</text>
          <text x="102" y="675" class="svlabel" font-size="14">Dual-path growth engine</text>
          <g font-size="10">
            <rect x="60" y="692" width="250" height="36" rx="9" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="74" y="714" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Low-ticket buyers → ascension track</text>
            <rect x="318" y="692" width="250" height="36" rx="9" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="332" y="714" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Non-buyers → new Part 1 each month</text>
            <rect x="576" y="692" width="266" height="36" rx="9" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="590" y="714" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Premium buyers → referral + expansion</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>Notice what's missing: a date.</b> Nothing here expires, which is why a reverse squeeze costs fewer hours to run each week — and why it takes longer to produce a result. You're trading urgency for compounding.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="tbl"><table>
        <thead><tr><th>Stage</th><th>What you build</th><th>The number that proves it</th></tr></thead>
        <tbody>
          <tr><td><b>S1 Traffic</b></td><td>2 variants per channel, all pointing at Part 1. No registration language anywhere — the click promise is the content itself</td><td class="num">Clicks to Part 1, per channel</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>S2 Part 1</b></td><td>The content page, the asset itself, depth events at 25/50/75/100%, and the opt-in bridge with its threshold in the spec</td><td class="num">% reaching 75% depth</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>S3 Nurture</b></td><td>Three depth-triggered sequences, not one time-triggered one. Each with its own hook</td><td class="num">Bridge opt-in rate per depth band</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>S4 Part 2</b></td><td>Gated page, instant delivery, email backup, and the escalation into the offer</td><td class="num">Part 2 completion rate</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>S5 Offer</b></td><td>Two offer pages — low-ticket and premium — plus the routing rule that decides which one they see</td><td class="num">Purchase rate, split by branch</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>S6 Growth</b></td><td>Three tracks: ascension for low-ticket buyers, a fresh Part 1 monthly for non-buyers, referral for premium buyers</td><td class="num">Repeat purchase and referral rate</td></tr>
        </tbody>
      </table></div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Pro tip · the threshold is your highest-leverage variable</div>
        <p>Where you place the opt-in bridge decides everything downstream. Too early and you're a squeeze page wearing a costume — you lose the trust the format exists to build. Too late and people finish, feel satisfied, and leave. <strong>Put it in the spec, start around three-quarters through, and treat it as your first A/B test.</strong></p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 3.7</span><b>If You're Building a Reverse Squeeze, Build It in This Order</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Make Part 1 first</strong>, and make it genuinely good enough that you'd have charged for it. This is not negotiable — the entire mechanism rests on the value being real.</li>
            <li><strong>Instrument depth before you build anything else.</strong> Without depth events you have no funnel, just a page.</li>
            <li><strong>Build the bridge</strong> and set its threshold in the spec, not in code.</li>
            <li><strong>Build Part 2</strong> and the offer inside it.</li>
            <li><strong>Then</strong> the three nurture branches, then the two offer pages and the routing rule.</li>
            <li><strong>Traffic last.</strong> Same as every other shape.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You</span><span><i>Tool</i>Claude Code · video host · email</span><span><i>Time</i>33 h build · 4–8 h/week to run</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Part 1 · bridge · Part 2 · 3 branches · 2 offer pages</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Depth events fire at all four milestones for a real session</span></div>

      <div class="chap"><span class="no">Day 3</span><span class="tag">Launch the AI RevOps Engine · Test It, Then Grow It</span></div>
      <h2>Make an AI Run the Whole Thing — Then Prove It Works</h2>
      <p class="lead">Today the funnel stops being a stack of files and becomes a <em>living system</em>: built by an agent, instrumented end to end, wired into a CRM and a set of automations, and then pointed at a small amount of real traffic to find out whether any of it is true.</p>
      <p>We'll use <strong>Claude Code</strong> as the worked example throughout. The system is AI-agnostic — the same context and the same prompts work in any agentic coding tool. What matters is the <em>ritual</em>, not the vendor.</p>
      <h3 id="d3-step-1-the-build-ritual">Step 1 — The Build Ritual</h3>
      <p>Here's the deal: the difference between an AI that builds you a funnel and an AI that builds you a generic template is <strong>what you give it before you ask.</strong> Handing an agent "build me a funnel" gets you a template. Handing it your conversion engine, your spec outline and a build checklist gets you <em>your</em> funnel.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Eight phases — and the agent refuses to skip ahead</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 50 860 436" role="img" aria-label="The build ritual as eight phases: load the context, run the interview, author and approve the spec, scaffold and theme, build the funnel core, wire commerce and lifecycle, instrument every step, then quality-assure and deploy. A gate between phase two and three shows that no code is written until the spec is approved. A feedback arrow returns from deploy to the spec.">

            <text x="446" y="118" class="svwhite2" font-size="9.5">Nothing is built until you say yes.</text>
            <rect x="624" y="64" width="196" height="64" rx="11" fill="var(--accent-2)"/>
            <text x="642" y="86" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">4 · Scaffold + theme</text>
            <text x="642" y="104" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">one project, one spec file,</text>
            <text x="642" y="118" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">brand tokens bound to variables</text>
            <rect x="16" y="236" width="196" height="64" rx="11" fill="var(--accent-2)"/>
            <text x="34" y="258" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">5 · Build the core</text>
            <text x="34" y="276" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">every screen the spec declares,</text>
            <text x="34" y="290" class="svmute" font-size="9">rendered from the spec, not by hand</text>
            <rect x="232" y="236" width="196" height="64" rx="11" fill="var(--accent-2)"/>
            <text x="250" y="258" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">6 · Commerce + lifecycle</text>
            <text x="250" y="276" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">checkout · webhook verified</text>
            <text x="250" y="290" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">server-side · onboarding triggered</text>
            <rect x="428" y="236" width="196" height="64" rx="11" fill="#0f9e75"/>

            <text x="446" y="290" class="svwhite2" font-size="9.5">per event. No step left dark.</text>
            <rect x="624" y="236" width="196" height="64" rx="11" fill="var(--accent-2)"/>

            <text x="642" y="276" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">375 / 768 / 1280px · live URL</text>
            <text x="642" y="290" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">· a one-page operator guide</text>

          <path d="M852 344 C852 356 844 362 832 362 L60 362 C40 362 32 356 32 344 L32 320" fill="none" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-width="1.8" stroke-dasharray="6 6" marker-end="url(#b2)" class="flowline"/>
          <text x="440" y="356" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10" style="paint-order:stroke;stroke:var(--panel);stroke-width:7px;stroke-linejoin:round">every later change goes back through the spec — never straight into the code</text>
          <rect x="16" y="384" width="828" height="88" rx="12" fill="var(--panel-2)" stroke="var(--line-2)"/>
          <text x="36" y="408" class="svlabel" font-size="11.5">THE SIX RULES THE AGENT IS GIVEN UP FRONT</text>
          <text x="36" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="10">1 · Build the funnel core first, traffic second.   2 · Traffic is upstream and variable — it only feeds one URL.</text>
          <text x="36" y="444" class="svmute" font-size="10">3 · Every metric must be real and fetchable — never model with invented conversion rates.   4 · Keep every stage; skip nothing.</text>
          <text x="36" y="460" class="svmute" font-size="10">5 · One spec file drives everything; nothing per-brand is hardcoded.   6 · Build the optimisation loop, not just the funnel.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>Phase 3 is the whole trick.</b> An agent that restates your spec before building gives you one cheap chance to catch a misunderstanding. An agent that starts coding immediately gives you an expensive one, later.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Do this · make the agent interview you</div>
        <p>Your first message should <strong>forbid code</strong>. Load the context, then say: <em>"Read these in full. Confirm you've read them. Then interview me — brand and story, the diagnostic, offer and pricing, traffic channels, tech and deploy target — a few questions at a time, and wait for my answers. Restate the complete spec for my approval. Do not write any code until I approve it."</em></p>
        <p>It feels slower. It is dramatically faster, because you are not debugging a funnel built on a guess.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 4.1</span><b>Run the Ritual End to End</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li>Put your Day-2 engine, your spec outline and your Day-3 asset list in one folder.</li>
            <li>Open your agent <em>in that folder</em> so it can read them.</li>
            <li>Send the no-code-yet prompt. Answer the interview honestly — "I don't know yet" is a valid answer and better than a made-up one.</li>
            <li>Read the spec it restates. <strong>Actually read it.</strong> This is the cheapest review you will ever do.</li>
            <li>Approve, then let it build, showing you each stage as it lands.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · agent builds</span><span><i>Tool</i>Claude Code, or any agentic tool</span><span><i>Time</i>3–5 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>A live URL and a spec file you can edit</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>You change a price in the spec and the live page updates</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-1-the-build-ritual"></div>
<h3 id="d3-what-the-orchestrator-actually-is">What the Orchestrator Actually Is</h3>
      <p>Once it's built, the agent doesn't go away. It keeps the context — your offer, your ICP, your pages, your templates, your numbers — and becomes the thing you and your team ask questions of and give instructions to.</p>
<div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">The RevOps orchestrator — context in, operations out</div>

            <rect x="8" y="98" width="150" height="42" rx="10" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="24" y="114" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="12.5">Pages + templates</text><text x="24" y="129" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">from Day 2</text>

            <rect x="648" y="223" width="146" height="48" rx="10" fill="var(--teal-bg)"/><text x="664" y="242" fill="var(--teal)" font-weight="700" font-size="12.5">Instant answers</text><text x="664" y="258" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">knowledge on tap</text>

          <path d="M303 256 L303 300 L470 300" fill="none" stroke="var(--amber)" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-dasharray="4 4" marker-end="url(#mkOffer2)" opacity=".7"/>
          <rect x="470" y="286" width="322" height="30" rx="15" fill="var(--amber-bg)"/><text x="486" y="302" fill="var(--amber)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">Any team question → answered in 2 seconds</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap"><b>The retention unlock:</b> a central orchestrator that holds all your funnel knowledge is what turns a pile of files into a system anyone on the team can query instantly.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box why"><div class="h">◆ The real unlock — most people get this wrong</div>
        <p>The biggest problem isn't making the orchestrator "perfect." You can refine that forever. The real problem is <strong>instant access to every piece of knowledge in your funnel.</strong> When anyone on your team can ask "what's our offer for cold LinkedIn leads?" and get an answer in two seconds, that speed becomes a retention advantage. Knowledge you can reach beats knowledge you merely have.</p></div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-what-the-orchestrator-actually-is"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-2-instrument-every-step-or-the-second-half">Step 2 — Instrument Every Step, or the Second Half of Today Does Not Work</h3>
      <p>This is the part people skip, and it's the part that decides whether you have a funnel you can improve or a funnel you can only rebuild. <strong>Every step fires one event. The count of that event is the metric. A rate is one count divided by another.</strong></p>
      <div class="tbl"><table>
        <thead><tr><th>#</th><th>Event</th><th>Fires when</th><th>Where the count comes from</th></tr></thead>
        <tbody>
          <tr><td class="num">1</td><td><b>session</b></td><td>the entry page loads</td><td>Analytics + your own event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">2</td><td><b>start</b></td><td>first interaction — first field, first question, first play</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">3</td><td><b>qualify_complete</b></td><td>the qualification questions are answered</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">4</td><td><b>step_answer</b></td><td>each individual question or milestone — carries its own id</td><td>Event store + session recording</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">5</td><td><b>engage_complete</b></td><td>they finish the main body — the assessment, the session, Part 1</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">6</td><td><b>goals_complete</b></td><td>goals and history captured</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">7</td><td><b>result_view</b></td><td>the personalised result or offer reveal is shown</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">8</td><td><b>offer_view</b></td><td>the pricing or plan screen is shown</td><td>Analytics + event store</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">9</td><td><b>checkout_start</b></td><td>checkout opens</td><td>Analytics + payment provider</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">10</td><td><b>purchase</b></td><td>the charge succeeds — confirmed server-side</td><td>Payment API + CRM</td></tr>
          <tr><td class="num">11</td><td><b>upsell_take</b></td><td>an add-on is accepted</td><td>Payment API + CRM</td></tr>
        </tbody>
      </table></div>
      <div class="srcnote"><b>Two destinations, always.</b> Send every event to your analytics platform <em>and</em> to a first-party endpoint you control. Analytics gives you the exploration tools; your own store means you still have the data when a platform changes its API, its retention window, or its price.</div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Pro tip · event number 4 is the one that pays for itself</div>
        <p>Per-question and per-milestone drop-off is how you find out that <em>question seven</em> is costing you a fifth of your completions. Aggregate completion rate tells you something is wrong. Per-step drop-off tells you <strong>exactly what to delete.</strong></p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 4.2</span><b>Fire All Eleven, Then Prove They Arrive</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Instrument, then verify — the verification is the task.</p>
          <ol>
            <li>Install analytics and a session-recording tool. Both have free tiers; there is no excuse.</li>
            <li>Fire every event to both destinations, with the UTM attached so buyers map back to a channel.</li>
            <li><strong>Walk the funnel yourself, end to end, on a phone</strong>, and watch the events land in real time.</li>
            <li>Confirm each one is queryable through an API — not just visible in a dashboard. If an agent can't fetch it headlessly, your weekly loop will be manual forever.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · agent wires it</span><span><i>Tool</i>Analytics · recordings · your event endpoint</span><span><i>Time</i>2–3 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>11 events live, queryable by API</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>One real session produces all 11 in order</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-2-instrument-every-step-or-the-second-half"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-3-wire-the-pipeline">Step 3 — Wire the Pipeline</h3>
      <p>Events tell you what happened on a page. The pipeline tells you what happened to a <em>person</em>. You need both, and the pipeline is what makes forecasting possible.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Eleven stages, two exits, and the automation that guards each one</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 596" role="img" aria-label="An eleven-stage sales pipeline drawn as a descending chain: form filled, evaluation call scheduled, qualified, sales call held, follow-up, proposal sent, proposal accepted, payment completed, onboarding, and delivery. Two side exits branch off: no-show with a rebooking recovery, and unqualified into a thirty-day nurture. Each stage names the automation that guards it, and a conversion percentage sits on each transition.">

<h3 id="d3-step-4-the-eight-automations">Step 4 — The Eight Automations</h3>
      <p>These are the ones that carry the load. Each is a <strong>trigger, a condition and an action</strong> — and each one replaces a thing a human would otherwise forget to do.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Trigger → condition → action, eight times</div>

            <rect x="588" y="260" width="256" height="36" rx="8" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="602" y="276" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Apply the tag · update the row</text><text x="602" y="290" class="svmute" font-size="9">this is what the four tags are for</text>
            <rect x="16" y="332" width="284" height="36" rx="8" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="30" y="348" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">5 · You run the outreach import</text><text x="30" y="362" class="svmute" font-size="9">manual, from your outreach sheet</text>
            <rect x="322" y="332" width="244" height="36" rx="8" class="node"/><text x="336" y="348" class="svsub">contact exists?</text><text x="336" y="361" class="svmute" font-size="9">update if yes, create if no</text>
            <rect x="588" y="332" width="256" height="36" rx="8" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="602" y="348" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Write back status + contact id</text><text x="602" y="362" class="svmute" font-size="9">so the sheet never double-sends</text>
            <rect x="16" y="404" width="284" height="36" rx="8" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="30" y="420" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">6 · A tag is added to a contact</text><text x="30" y="434" class="svmute" font-size="9">or an appointment is confirmed</text>
            <rect x="322" y="404" width="244" height="36" rx="8" class="node"/><text x="336" y="420" class="svsub">match tag → stage</text><text x="336" y="433" class="svmute" font-size="9">remove the trigger tag after</text>
            <rect x="588" y="404" width="256" height="36" rx="8" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="602" y="420" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Move the pipeline stage</text><text x="602" y="434" class="svmute" font-size="9">removing the tag prevents re-firing</text>
            <rect x="16" y="476" width="284" height="36" rx="8" fill="var(--amber-bg)"/><text x="30" y="492" fill="var(--amber)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">7 · Inbox poll · every 60 seconds</text><text x="30" y="506" class="svmute" font-size="9">replies and delivery failures</text>
            <rect x="322" y="476" width="244" height="36" rx="8" class="node"/><text x="336" y="492" class="svsub">parse: accepted / declined /</text><text x="336" y="505" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">tentative / bounced</text>
            <rect x="588" y="476" width="256" height="36" rx="8" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="602" y="492" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Set reply status · flag bounces</text><text x="602" y="506" class="svmute" font-size="9">a bounce you don't record gets re-sent</text>
            <rect x="16" y="548" width="284" height="36" rx="8" fill="var(--amber-bg)"/><text x="30" y="564" fill="var(--amber)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">8 · Scheduler · every 5 minutes</text><text x="30" y="578" class="svmute" font-size="9">compares now against event time</text>
            <rect x="322" y="548" width="244" height="36" rx="8" class="node"/><text x="336" y="564" class="svsub">inside a window, ±3 min</text><text x="336" y="577" class="svmute" font-size="9">24h · 1h · 15m · live · +1/2/3 days</text>
            <rect x="588" y="548" width="256" height="36" rx="8" class="node" stroke="var(--teal)"/><text x="602" y="564" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Tag the contact → messaging sends</text><text x="602" y="578" class="svmute" font-size="9">wait-nodes between sends, or you get flagged</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap">Automations 1–6 are event-driven and fire instantly. 7 and 8 are polled, because inboxes and clocks don't send webhooks — and the polling interval is a cost decision, not a technical one.<br><b>Build them in this order.</b> 1, 3 and 4 are the minimum viable set — confirm, capture, and tag. Everything else is leverage you add once the first three are provably reliable.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 4.3</span><b>Wire the Pipeline and the First Three Automations</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>Don't try to build all eight today. Build the three that stop you losing data, and test each one with a real contact before moving on.</p>
          <ul>
            <li><strong>Custom fields first</strong> — source, UTM, the four tags, score and band. Adding a field later means back-filling every contact you've already captured.</li>
            <li><strong>Then the pipeline stages</strong>, with the trigger for each transition written down next to it. A stage with no trigger is a stage you'll be updating by hand.</li>
            <li><strong>Then automations 1, 3 and 4.</strong> Register yourself. Attend. Then register again and deliberately no-show. Confirm the tags land correctly both times.</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · agent wires it</span><span><i>Tool</i>Your CRM + automation tool</span><span><i>Time</i>4–6 h</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Pipeline + fields + 3 live automations</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>A deliberate no-show gets the no-show tag without you touching anything</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · the automations</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>All eight apply as written. Automation 8 is the heaviest lifter — the timed reminder engine is what carries show-up to 89%.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Add a ninth: the <em>daily generation loop</em>. Survey submitted → agent generates the day's output → PDF and link delivered. It runs once a day, three days running, and it is the product.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Automation 8 disappears entirely — there's no clock. It's replaced by depth-threshold triggers, which fire per person rather than per schedule.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> tripwire and free-plus-shipping funnels need a <b>fulfilment trigger</b> on purchase · product launches need a hard <b>cart-close job</b> that actually disables checkout · JV funnels need <b>attribution and payout splitting</b> at the moment of sale, because reconciling it later is how partnerships end.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-4-the-eight-automations"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-5-point-real-traffic-at-it">Step 5 — Point Real Traffic at It</h3>
      <p>Everything up to now is a hypothesis. The only thing that turns it into knowledge is strangers. Today you run a <strong>deliberately small test</strong> — small enough that being wrong is cheap, big enough that the result isn't noise.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
