        <div class="cap"><b>The point of today's traffic is not sales.</b> It's to find out which of yesterday's assumptions was wrong, while being wrong is still cheap.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 4.4</span><b>Run the Test and Write Down What Broke</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li><strong>QA first.</strong> Every page at 375, 768 and 1280px. A real purchase. Every event firing. Do not send traffic to a funnel you haven't walked yourself.</li>
            <li><strong>Launch the smallest version of each live channel</strong>, all UTM-tagged, all pointing at one URL.</li>
            <li><strong>Wait.</strong> Reading numbers after four hours is how people kill things that were working.</li>
            <li><strong>Pull both reports</strong> and find the single worst step. Not the worst three. The worst one.</li>
            <li><strong>Write down what surprised you.</strong> That sentence is the most valuable output of the entire day.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You</span><span><i>Tool</i>Your channels + both reports</span><span><i>Time</i>2 h to launch, then wait</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Live traffic, both reports, one named bottleneck</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Every buyer traces back to a channel via their UTM</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Your Day 3 output — half one</div>
        <p>A live funnel at a real URL, built from a spec you can edit, with all eleven events firing to two destinations, a pipeline that updates itself, the three critical automations running, real traffic flowing through it, and both reports assembled — plus one named bottleneck to attack tomorrow.</p></div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Tonight's homework · 20 minutes</div>
        <p>Let the traffic run overnight. Don't touch anything. Tomorrow is about reading what it tells you and deciding what to change — and you cannot read a signal you keep interrupting.</p></div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-5-point-real-traffic-at-it"></div>
<div class="half" id="d3b">
        <span class="hn">Half two · grow it</span>
        <h2 class="hh" id="d3-read-the-numbers-fix-the-worst-one-repeat">Read the Numbers, Fix the Worst One, Repeat</h2>
        <p class="hl">You have a funnel and you have data. This half is the discipline that turns those into a business: know your unit economics, find the single worst step, change one thing, measure, keep or revert. Every week, forever.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="box why"><div class="h">◆ Why one leak at a time</div>
        <p>Fixing five things at once feels productive and teaches you nothing — if the number moves you can't say which change did it, and if it drops you can't say which to undo. <strong>One change per leak is slower per week and vastly faster per quarter.</strong></p></div>
      <h3 id="d3-step-1-know-what-a-customer-costs-and-what-they">Step 1 — Know What a Customer Costs and What They're Worth</h3>
      <p>Two numbers decide whether you have a business or an expensive hobby: <strong>what it costs to acquire a customer</strong>, and <strong>what that customer is worth over their life.</strong> If the second is comfortably bigger than the first, you can buy growth. If it isn't, more traffic just loses money faster.</p>
      <div class="calc" id="calc">
        <div class="row"><label>Monthly visitors</label><input type="range" id="c1" min="500" max="50000" step="500" value="10000"><span class="out" id="o1">10,000</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Opt-in rate</label><input type="range" id="c2" min="5" max="60" step="1" value="25"><span class="out" id="o2">25%</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Show-up / engage</label><input type="range" id="c3" min="10" max="95" step="1" value="40"><span class="out" id="o3">40%</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Buy rate</label><input type="range" id="c4" min="1" max="40" step="1" value="8"><span class="out" id="o4">8%</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Price</label><input type="range" id="c5" min="49" max="9999" step="50" value="999"><span class="out" id="o5">$999</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Monthly spend</label><input type="range" id="c6" min="0" max="50000" step="500" value="6000"><span class="out" id="o6">$6,000</span></div>
        <div class="row"><label>Repeat purchases</label><input type="range" id="c7" min="0" max="400" step="10" value="60"><span class="out" id="o7">0.6×</span></div>
        <div class="res">
          <div class="b"><div class="k">Buyers / month</div><div class="v" id="rBuyers">—</div></div>
          <div class="b g"><div class="k">Annual run-rate</div><div class="v" id="rARR">—</div></div>
          <div class="b"><div class="k">Cost per buyer (CAC)</div><div class="v" id="rCAC">—</div></div>
          <div class="b g"><div class="k">Lifetime value (LTV)</div><div class="v" id="rLTV">—</div></div>
          <div class="b"><div class="k">LTV ÷ CAC</div><div class="v" id="rRatio">—</div></div>
          <div class="b g"><div class="k">Return on spend</div><div class="v" id="rROAS">—</div></div>
        </div>
        <div class="calcnote" id="calcVerdict"></div>
      </div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Pro tip · the ratio, not the number</div>
        <p>A $600 cost per customer is not "high" or "low" on its own. Against a $400 lifetime value it's a business that dies; against a $4,000 one it's a business you should be pouring money into. <strong>Watch the ratio.</strong> Roughly 3× is the conventional floor for a healthy funnel, and below about 1× you are paying for the privilege of having customers.</p></div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Do this · replace every slider with your own fetched number</div>
        <p>The sliders are for building intuition. The real version has your numbers in it, pulled from the reports you assembled yesterday — and any term you still can't fetch stays marked <em>unknown</em> until you instrument it. A model built on plausible-looking guesses is worse than no model, because you'll trust it.</p></div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-1-know-what-a-customer-costs-and-what-they"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-2-find-the-leak-then-fix-the-right-thing">Step 2 — Find the Leak, Then Fix the Right Thing</h3>
      <p>Compare every step rate to the rest of <em>your</em> funnel, and to itself last week. The lowest one — or the one that dropped — is where you work. Here's what each leak usually means and what to change.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Leak diagnosis — the symptom, the likely cause, the one thing to change</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 584" role="img" aria-label="Leak diagnosis matrix with eight rows. Each row names a symptom, the likely cause, and the single element to experiment on: entry page not earning the start, drop-off on specific questions, low completion overall, the result not earning the click to price, the offer not landing, checkout friction, weak upsell take, and high channel cost per buyer.">
          <text x="16" y="22" class="svsub" font-size="11">WORK TOP TO BOTTOM — THE FIRST ROW THAT MATCHES IS YOUR WEEK</text>
          <line x1="16" y1="34" x2="844" y2="34" stroke="var(--line-2)" stroke-width="1.5"/>
          <text x="32" y="50" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">THE SYMPTOM</text>
          <text x="316" y="50" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">WHAT IT USUALLY MEANS</text>
          <text x="590" y="50" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">CHANGE EXACTLY THIS</text>
          <g font-size="10">
            <rect x="16" y="60" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel-2)"/>
            <text x="32" y="80" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Visits, but almost no starts</text><text x="32" y="96" class="svmute" font-size="9">session → start is your lowest rate</text>
            <text x="316" y="88" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">The page isn't earning the first click</text>
            <text x="590" y="80" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Headline · the time promise · the hero</text><text x="590" y="96" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">image · page speed · CTA above the fold</text>
            <rect x="16" y="128" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel)"/>
            <text x="32" y="148" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">A cliff at one specific question</text><text x="32" y="164" class="svmute" font-size="9">visible only in per-step drop-off</text>
            <text x="316" y="156" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">That question creates friction or confusion</text>
            <text x="590" y="148" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Cut it, reorder it, or reword it. Add a</text><text x="590" y="164" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">breather screen before it.</text>
            <rect x="16" y="196" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel-2)"/>
            <text x="32" y="216" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Low completion across the board</text><text x="32" y="232" class="svmute" font-size="9">no single cliff, everyone thins out</text>
            <text x="316" y="224" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">It's simply too long</text>
            <text x="590" y="216" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Cut the count. Show progress. Tighten</text><text x="590" y="232" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">the clusters. Shorter almost always wins.</text>
            <rect x="16" y="264" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel)"/>
            <text x="32" y="284" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">They see the result, not the price</text><text x="32" y="300" class="svmute" font-size="9">result → offer view is low</text>
            <text x="316" y="292" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">The result doesn't earn the next click</text>
            <text x="590" y="284" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">How the score is presented · echoing their</text><text x="590" y="300" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">own words back · the CTA label itself</text>
            <rect x="16" y="332" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel-2)"/>
            <text x="32" y="352" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">They see the price and stop</text><text x="32" y="368" class="svmute" font-size="9">offer view → checkout start is low</text>
            <text x="316" y="360" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">The offer isn't landing, or the price isn't framed</text>
            <text x="590" y="352" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Tier framing · anchoring · which tier is</text><text x="590" y="368" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">preselected · the guarantee · renewal clarity</text>
            <rect x="16" y="400" width="828" height="62" rx="8" fill="var(--panel)"/>
            <text x="32" y="420" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">They start checkout and vanish</text><text x="32" y="436" class="svmute" font-size="9">the most expensive leak you have</text>
            <text x="316" y="428" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Friction or a trust gap at the final step</text>
            <text x="590" y="420" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Wallets first · fewer fields · trust strip ·</text><text x="590" y="436" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">fix auth failures · correct currency and locale</text>
            <rect x="16" y="468" width="828" height="50" rx="8" fill="var(--panel-2)"/>
            <text x="32" y="488" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">Nobody takes the upsell</text><text x="32" y="504" class="svmute" font-size="9">free money you're leaving behind</text>
            <text x="316" y="496" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Wrong offer, wrong price, or wrong order</text>
            <text x="590" y="496" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">The offer itself · its price · the ladder order</text>
            <rect x="16" y="524" width="828" height="50" rx="8" fill="var(--pink-bg)"/>
            <text x="32" y="544" fill="var(--pink)" font-weight="700" font-size="10.5">One channel's cost per buyer is high</text><text x="32" y="560" class="svmute" font-size="9">the funnel is fine — the traffic isn't</text>
            <text x="316" y="552" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">Weak creative, or the wrong list entirely</text>
            <text x="590" y="552" class="svsub" font-size="9.5">The hook and the list — or move the budget</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap">Note the difference between the last row and every other row: the first seven are <b>funnel</b> problems, the last is a <b>traffic</b> problem. Fixing the wrong category is the most common wasted week.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-2-find-the-leak-then-fix-the-right-thing"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-3-run-the-loop-every-week">Step 3 — Run the Loop Every Week</h3>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Six steps, one lap per week, forever</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 22 860 410" role="img" aria-label="The weekly optimisation loop drawn as a ring of six steps: observe, locate, hypothesise, experiment, measure, and keep or revert, with the ring returning to observe. Side panels list the daily, weekly, monthly and quarterly cadence and the four signs the loop is working.">
          <defs>
            <marker id="l1" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="8" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse"><path d="M1 1L8 5L1 9" fill="none" stroke="var(--accent)" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"/></marker>
          </defs>
          <circle cx="262" cy="232" r="150" fill="none" stroke="var(--accent)" stroke-width="2" stroke-dasharray="7 9" class="flowline" opacity=".5"/>
          <g font-size="10.5">
            <circle cx="262" cy="82" r="46" fill="var(--accent)"/><text x="262" y="78" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite" font-size="11.5">OBSERVE</text><text x="262" y="94" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite2" font-size="9">fetch both reports</text>
            <circle cx="392" cy="157" r="46" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="392" y="153" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">LOCATE</text><text x="392" y="169" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9">the single worst step</text>
            <circle cx="392" cy="307" r="46" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="392" y="299" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11">HYPOTH-</text><text x="392" y="313" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11">ESISE</text><text x="392" y="329" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9">one reason, one fix</text>
            <circle cx="262" cy="382" r="46" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="262" y="378" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11">EXPERIMENT</text><text x="262" y="394" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9">50 / 50, one change</text>
            <circle cx="132" cy="307" r="46" fill="var(--accent-2)"/><text x="132" y="303" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--accent-ink)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">MEASURE</text><text x="132" y="319" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9">wait for a real sample</text>
            <circle cx="132" cy="157" r="46" fill="#0f9e75"/><text x="132" y="150" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite" font-size="11">KEEP OR</text><text x="132" y="164" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite" font-size="11">REVERT</text><text x="132" y="180" text-anchor="middle" class="svwhite2" font-size="9">then log it</text>
          </g>
          <text x="262" y="226" text-anchor="middle" class="svlabel" font-size="12">ONE LAP</text>
          <text x="262" y="244" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">per week</text>
          <rect x="452" y="40" width="392" height="184" rx="12" class="node"/>
          <text x="472" y="66" class="svlabel" font-size="11.5">THE CADENCE</text>
          <g font-size="10">
            <text x="472" y="90" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">Daily · automated</text><text x="472" y="106" class="svmute">fetch both reports; alert if a step rate falls sharply</text>
            <text x="472" y="124" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">Weekly · you and the agent</text><text x="472" y="140" class="svmute">confirm the bottleneck, launch one experiment, read last week's</text>
            <text x="472" y="158" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">Monthly</text><text x="472" y="174" class="svmute">re-pull spend, rebalance budget, prune dead questions and upsells</text>
            <text x="472" y="192" class="svsub" font-size="10.5">Quarterly</text><text x="472" y="208" class="svmute">revisit the diagnostic itself — are these still the right axes?</text>
          </g>
          <rect x="452" y="240" width="392" height="184" rx="12" fill="var(--teal-bg)"/>
          <text x="472" y="266" fill="var(--teal)" font-weight="700" font-size="11.5">YOU'RE DOING IT RIGHT WHEN</text>
          <g font-size="10.5" class="svsub">
            <text x="472" y="292">· Every step rate is fetched, never guessed</text>
            <text x="472" y="316">· You know today's bottleneck without thinking</text>
            <text x="472" y="340">· Every buyer maps to a channel, and budget</text>
            <text x="486" y="356">follows cost-per-buyer, not cost-per-click</text>
            <text x="472" y="380">· Exactly one experiment runs per leak</text>
            <text x="472" y="404">· This month's worst step beats last month's</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap">The loop can run semi-autonomously: the agent fetches both reports on a schedule, flags the worst leak and proposes a variant — you still decide what ships.<br><b>And the bottleneck always moves.</b> Fix the worst step and a different one becomes the worst. That's the loop working, and it's why this is a weekly habit, not a project with an end date.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 5.1</span><b>Run One Full Lap Today</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <ol>
            <li><strong>Observe</strong> — pull both reports from yesterday's traffic.</li>
            <li><strong>Locate</strong> — name the single worst step out loud. Write it down.</li>
            <li><strong>Hypothesise</strong> — one sentence: <em>"I think it leaks because ___, so I'll change ___."</em></li>
            <li><strong>Experiment</strong> — put the variant in the spec, serve it to half your sessions, keep everything else identical.</li>
            <li><strong>Diary it</strong> — the change, the date, the metric you expect to move, and what you'll do either way. Future you will not remember.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You · agent fetches</span><span><i>Tool</i>Both reports + your spec</span><span><i>Time</i>60 min</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>One live experiment + an experiment log</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Both arms are being served and counted separately</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-3-run-the-loop-every-week"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-4-move-the-money-to-the-channel-that-actual">Step 4 — Move the Money to the Channel That Actually Buys</h3>
      <p>Here's the rule that saves the most money: <strong>reallocate toward the lowest cost per buyer, not the lowest cost per click.</strong> A cheap click that never converts is the most expensive traffic you own.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">Same spend, redistributed by cost per buyer</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 452" role="img" aria-label="Channel budget reallocation. Five channels each show spend, buyers and cost per buyer, with a before-and-after budget bar. Budget shifts away from the highest cost per buyer toward the lowest, holding total spend constant, producing more buyers for the same money.">
          <text x="16" y="22" class="svsub" font-size="11">READ THE COST PER BUYER COLUMN FIRST — EVERYTHING ELSE IS DECORATION</text>

          <text x="36" y="428" class="svmute" font-size="10.5">costing $57–109. This is the highest-return hour of the month, and most people never spend it because the report isn't built. Yours is.</text>
        </svg>
        <div class="cap">Figures are a teaching model. <b>The method is not:</b> rank by cost per buyer, fund the top, starve the bottom, keep a small test budget on everything so you'd notice if it changed.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box tip"><div class="h">◆ Pro tip · never cut a channel to zero</div>
        <p>Leave a small always-on test budget everywhere. Channels change — an audience gets cheaper, a platform changes its algorithm, a competitor stops bidding. If you cut to zero you'll never find out, and you'll be the last to know when the cheapest channel in your market becomes available again.</p></div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-4-move-the-money-to-the-channel-that-actual"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-5-the-52-week-engine">Step 5 — The 52-Week Engine</h3>
      <p>Most of your list won't buy this month. The engine is what keeps them warm until the month they do — and what turns your existing customers into the cheapest traffic you'll ever get.</p>
      <div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">A year of contact, on four rails, from one topic rotation</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 860 512" role="img" aria-label="The fifty-two week growth engine. A reactivation branch returns lapsed contacts to the top of the funnel.">
          <defs>
            <marker id="g1" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="8" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse"><path d="M1 1L8 5L1 9" fill="none" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-width="1.8" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"/></marker>
          </defs>
          <text x="16" y="22" class="svsub" font-size="11">ONE TOPIC ROTATION · FOUR RAILS · FOUR SEGMENTS</text>

          <text x="114" y="96" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">across every rail and segment</text>

            <rect x="480" y="108" width="364" height="48" rx="9" fill="var(--panel-2)"/>

          </g>

          <text x="420" y="292" text-anchor="middle" class="svmute" font-size="10">advocates and reactivated contacts re-enter at the top of the funnel — this is what makes it an engine rather than a broadcast</text>

          <g font-size="10.5">

            <text x="32" y="388" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">1 · Segment or die</text>
            <text x="32" y="410" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">Sending everyone the same message is</text>
            <text x="32" y="426" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">how a list stops opening. The four tags</text>
            <text x="32" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">from Day 2 are the segmentation — you</text>
            <text x="32" y="458" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">already built it, so use it.</text>
            <rect x="296" y="364" width="268" height="118" rx="11" class="node"/>
            <text x="312" y="388" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">2 · Value before ask, always</text>
            <text x="312" y="410" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">A rough ratio: four useful sends for</text>
            <text x="312" y="426" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">every one that asks for something.</text>
            <text x="312" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">Invert it and your open rate decays</text>
            <text x="312" y="458" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">until the list is worthless.</text>
            <rect x="576" y="364" width="268" height="118" rx="11" class="node"/>
            <text x="592" y="388" class="svlabel" font-size="10.5">3 · Let people leave quietly</text>
            <text x="592" y="410" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">A visible unsubscribe and a "less often"</text>
            <text x="592" y="426" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">option protect deliverability for</text>
            <text x="592" y="442" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">everyone else. People who can't leave</text>
            <text x="592" y="458" class="svmute" font-size="9.5">mark you as spam instead.</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
      </div>
      <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-5-the-52-week-engine"></div>
<h3 id="d3-step-6-where-1m-arr-actually-comes-from">Step 6 — Where $1M ARR Actually Comes From</h3>
      <p>Not from one enormous month. From a wheel that gets slightly easier to turn each time: a new customer joins, gets a fast win, asks for more, gets it, stays, and refers. Every turn adds recurring revenue and lowers the cost of the next customer.</p>
<div class="viz wide">
        <div class="vtitle">The flywheel — customers never exit, they re-enter</div>
        <svg viewBox="0 0 720 506" role="img" aria-label="RevOps flywheel: three arcs — attract, engage, delight — rotate around RevOps at the hub; retained customers re-enter through referrals, renewals and expansion.">
          <defs>

        <div class="legend2"><span><i style="background:#6366f1"></i>Attract</span><span><i style="background:#9333ea"></i>Engage</span><span><i style="background:#0f9e75"></i>Delight</span><span><i style="background:var(--accent)"></i>RevOps at the hub</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="tbl"><table>
        <thead><tr><th>Lever</th><th>What you change</th><th>Why it compounds</th></tr></thead>
        <tbody>
          <tr><td><b>More buyers</b></td><td>Fix the worst funnel step; move budget to the lowest cost per buyer</td><td>Linear — real, but it stops the moment you stop spending</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>Higher price</b></td><td>Strengthen the value equation until the price is obviously fair</td><td>Immediate, and it flows into every calculation below</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>Ascension</b></td><td>Make sure the rung above exists, and that buyers are told about it</td><td>The cheapest revenue you will ever earn — they've already bought</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>Retention</b></td><td>The first 48 hours after purchase, and a fast first win</td><td>Multiplies every number above it. This is the actual north star</td></tr>
          <tr><td><b>Referral</b></td><td>Ask advocates directly, and make sharing trivially easy</td><td>Lowers acquisition cost, which raises the ratio, which funds more traffic</td></tr>
        </tbody>
      </table></div>
      <div class="box why"><div class="h">◆ Retention is the only lever that multiplies the others</div>
        <p>Doubling your traffic doubles your buyers. Doubling your retention doubles the value of <em>every</em> buyer you have ever acquired and every one you will acquire. That's why the north star here is recurring revenue, not the first sale — and why the 48 hours after someone pays deserve more of your attention than the 48 days before.</p></div>
      <div class="task">
        <div class="th"><span class="tn">Task 5.2</span><b>Write the Scale Plan</b></div>
        <div class="tm">
          <p>One page, five lines. Then put the weekly review in your calendar as a recurring event, because a loop that isn't scheduled is a loop that runs twice and stops.</p>
          <ol>
            <li><strong>My cost per buyer is ___ and my lifetime value is ___.</strong> Both fetched, neither guessed.</li>
            <li><strong>My cheapest channel is ___, so next month's budget moves toward it.</strong></li>
            <li><strong>My worst step is ___ and this week's experiment is ___.</strong></li>
            <li><strong>My ascension offer is ___</strong> — the rung above what they just bought.</li>
            <li><strong>My retention mechanism is ___</strong> — what produces the first win, and by when.</li>
          </ol>
        </div>
        <div class="meta"><span><i>Owner</i>You</span><span><i>Tool</i>One page</span><span><i>Time</i>45 min</span><span class="dl"><i>Deliverable</i>Scale plan + a recurring weekly review</span><span class="pv"><i>Proof</i>Lines 1 and 2 contain fetched numbers, not estimates</span></div>
      </div>
      <div class="fvar">
        <div class="fh">◆ What changes per funnel type · scaling</div>
        <div class="fb">
          <div class="fc w"><b>Webinar Funnel</b><span>Scale by <em>frequency</em> — run it monthly rather than occasionally. The assets are already built, so each additional run costs mostly promotion. Watch show-up rate as volume grows; it usually dips first.</span></div>
          <div class="fc k"><b>Workshop · Challenge</b><span>Scale by <em>cohort size and cadence</em>, but completion rate is the constraint. A cohort too large to feel personal converts worse on day five — and day five is where the money is.</span></div>
          <div class="fc r"><b>Reverse Squeeze</b><span>Scale by <em>content volume</em>. Each new Part 1 is a permanent new entrance, and old ones keep producing. Slowest to start, best compounding of the three.</span></div>
        </div>
        <div class="fo"><b>Other types:</b> tripwire and free-plus-shipping funnels scale on <b>ad spend</b>, limited only by how fast the upsell recovers acquisition cost · community funnels scale on <b>member-driven referral</b> and stall if the community goes quiet · JV funnels scale by <b>adding partners</b>, so partner recruitment becomes the growth function.</div>
      </div>
      <div class="box do"><div class="h">◆ Your Day 3 output — half two</div>
        <p>Your scale plan: unit economics you fetched rather than guessed, a channel budget ranked by cost per buyer, one live experiment on your worst step, the 52-week engine segmented and scheduled, and a standing weekly review in the calendar.</p></div>
    <div class="vipslot" data-step="d3-step-6-where-1m-arr-actually-comes-from"></div>
